2.   RECOGNITION  
A. PAULA FLEMING – BOARD SERVICE 
Tonight, we have an opportunity to honor the commitment and dedication of a 
woman who has given so much of herself to the Troy School District.  Paula 
Fleming finished her 12 th year of service on the Troy Board of Education in June  
2012, and we would like to show our appreciation for this accomplishment. 
Throughout Paula’s 12 years  on the Board , she has been a tireless cheerleader 
for the Troy School District community.  She has brought passion, expertise and 
high expectations to every decision that has affected this community and its 
children.  Paula has worked hard to bring the entire Troy com munity together to 
build a district that is recognized for excellence at both the state and national 
levels.  
In Paula’s past year as trustee on the Board, the district has celebrated 
significant accomplishments in the classroom and  in academic, artistic and 
athletic competitions , nationwide. Our students have seen their experiments 
conducted at the International Space station. Our bands and orchestras have 
performed for thousands of people at venues across our country.  And, under the 
leadership of Paula and the rest of the Board , the entire district has  increased 
the number of NUMATS Midwest Talent Search winners, National Merit Semi -
finalists, and the number of students taking and pass ing A dvanced Placement 
courses.     
It is true that the strength of o ur district is the quality of the staff, students, 
parents and community that make up the Troy School District team.  Tonight, 
however, we want to make sure that at least one of our key players is 
recognized for her tireless efforts to enhance the opportunities for the children of 
our community.  Paula, we are proud to present you with this specially engraved 
chair, a token of appreciation for your years of service.   
________________________________________________ 
Recommended Action:  Present chair.

Advanced Placement Honors 
Troy School District 
Dr. Fowler explained to the Board that more than 400 
Troy students have been honored for their outstanding 
performance on Advanced Placement exams during the 
past school year.  Troy students were honored by the 
College Board in four award categories: AP Scholar, AP 
Scholar with Honors, AP Scholar with Distinction and 
National AP Scholar.  The Board was asked to recognize 
the students who have earned the National AP Scholar 
honors.  This award is granted to students who receive 
an average score of at least four on all of the AP exams 
that were taken last year.  Students in this category must 
also take eight or more AP exams during the school year 
and score a four or higher on these exams.  A number of 
those students were seniors last year.  Students who 
achieved this honor during their junior year were invited 
to be present at this evening’s meeting.  Those students

2012-2013 School Year 
Anne Mull provided the Board with an overview of 
reviewed some of the activities and learning opportunities 
that have been offered to  both teachers and the 
administrative staff.  She mentioned the different areas 
and topics covered with new teachers during New 
Teacher Orientation.  The areas of focus included 
diversity and inclusion in the Troy School District, 
effective instructional strategies, District technology 
resources, special education an d what it means to join a 
professional learning community.   
Mrs. Mull talked about some of  the activities initiated by 
the Diversity and Inclusion Council to help teachers 
understand and connect with students from different 
backgrounds. She mentioned examples of professional 
development that administrators participate in throughout 
the year.  She also talked about the recently developed 
Troy Educator Evaluation Model, which has been put in 
place to identify teachers needing support.  She also 
reviewed the District’s mentoring process for 
probationary teachers.  She then spoke about the 
upcoming NCA External Review for accreditation and the 
process associated with it.  She then addressed the 
Michigan Department of Education’s recent designation of 
Focus Schools and provided a background of what this 
means to our District and community. She shared how 
our administrators and teache rs are working together to 
help close our achievement gap. She reviewed examples 
of what the District is doing to address individual needs 
of our students in order to help them become successful 
learners.  Mrs. Mull then spoke briefly about the District’s 
recent collaboration with Peer Districts.   
determining how districts were considered the most alike.

well.

BUSINESS 
WHEREAS, Troy School District (TSD) owns and 
operates facilities within th e boundaries of the “Detroit” 
urbanized area which discharges storm water to a 
municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4); and  
WHEREAS, The Michigan Department of Environmental 
Quality – Water Bureau maintains oversight and 
regulatory authority for co mpliance with the terms and 
conditions of the NPDE S General Permit number 
MIS049000; and  
WHEREAS, Troy School District has applied for and 
entered into an Administrative Consent Agreement to 
comply with the requirements of NPDES General Permit 
number MIS049000 to discharge storm water from Troy 
School District facilities to the MS4; and  
WHEREAS, the conditions of General Permit number 
MIS049000 require Troy School District to develop and 
implement policies and procedures to ensure compliance 
with storm water discharges from new development and 
redevelopment of projects containing an area of 
disturbance exceeding 1 acre, and 
WHEREAS, T r o y  S c h o o l  D i s t r i c t  a g r e e s  t o  c o m p l y  w i t h  
the requirements of General Permit number MIS049000, 
and  
WHEREAS, Troy School District agrees to obtain a 
construction site permit from the local municipality or 
other governing unit for new development and 
redevelopment projects that disturb an area exceeding 1 
acre, and 
WHEREAS, Troy School District agrees to operate and 
maintain structural controls for the purpose of reducing 
pollutant contribution, control runoff, and decrease or 
eliminate stream bank erosion due to storm water runoff.  
THEREFORE, be it resolved that the Troy School District 
Board of Education is highly committed to practicing 
sound environmental principals  including the discharging

95
     8. A.        11.13.12 
TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
TROY, MICHIGAN 48098 
ANNUAL SUMMER TAX RESOLUTION 
 WHEREAS, this Board of Ed ucation by resolution of November 13, 2012, 
determined to impose a summer property tax levy to collect one -half of school property 
taxes, including debt services, upon property located within the Troy School  District, 
beginning with 20 13 and continuing from year to year until specifically revoked by this 
Board of Education. 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1.  This Board of Education, pursuant to 1976 P.A. 451, as amended, hereby 
invokes a summer tax levy of one -half of school property taxes, including d ebt service, 
continuing with 2013, and continuing from year to year until specifically revoked by this 
Board of Education, and requests each city in which this district is located and in which a 
local school district or city is concurrently imposing a summer tax levy to collect those 
summer taxes. 
 2.  The Superintendent or his/her designee is authorized and directed to 
forward to the governing body of each city in which this district is located and in which a 
local school district or city is concurrently imposing a summer tax levy, a copy of this 
Board’s resolution requesting that each such city agree to coll ect the summer tax levy 
for 2013 in the amount spe cified in this resolution.  Such forwarding of the resolution 
and the request to collect the summer tax levy shall be sent so that they are received by 
the appropriate governing bodies before January 1, 2013. 
 3.  The Superintendent or his/her designee is  authorized and directed to 
negotiate on behalf of this District with the governing body of each city in which the 
District is located for the reasonable expenses for collection of the District’s summer tax 
levy that the city may bill under MCLA 380.1612.  Any such proposed agreement shall be 
brought before this Board for its approval or disapproval. 
 4.  All resolutions and parts of resolutions insofar as they conflict with the 
provisions of this resolution be and the same are hereby rescinded. 
 Yes: Members 
 No: Members 
Resolution declared adopted.       _____________________________ 
  Secretary, Board of Education 
 The undersigned, duly qualified and acting Secretary of the Board of 
Education of Troy School District, Troy, Michigan, hereby certifies that the foregoing 
constitutes a true and complete copy of a resolution adopted by said Board of Education 
at a regular meeting held on November 13, 2013 , the original of which is part of the 
Board’s minutes.  The undersigned further certifies that notice of the meeting was given 
to the public pursuant to the provisions of the “Open Meetings Act” (Act 267, P .A. 1976, 
as amended).

Secretary, Board of Education

B. CONSIDERATION:  BEST PRACTICES INCENTIVE 
  Attached is a resolution for Board consideration regarding the Best 
Practices Incentive with the Michigan Department of Education. 
________________________________________________________ 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the resolution below. 
 Moved by:       __________________________ 
 Supported by:  __________________________ 
WHEREAS, Section 22f of the State School Aid Act provides $ 52 per pupil one-time grants 
to districts that satisfy at least 7 of 8 best practices criteria not later than June 1, 2013 (MCL 
388.1622f); and; 
WHEREAS, the Board of Education of Troy School District desires to receive the $ 52 per 
pupil incentive payment; and; 
WHEREAS, the Troy School District has satisfied at least 7 of 8 best practices criteria; and; 
WHEREAS, eligibility for the incentive payment is contingent upon adopting a resolution 
that states the District has complied with at least 7 of 8 of the best practices criteria;  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED AS FOLLOWS: 
A. The Board of Education of  Troy School District certifies that the District has 
complied with the following requirements: 
1) The District is the designated policy holder for medical benefit plan(s) 
pursuant to Section 22f(2)(a). 
2) The District has obtained a competitive bid on non -instructional services 
pursuant to Section 22f(2)(b). 
i. This includes custodial services, transportation and food services. 
3) The district accepts applications for enrollment by non -resident 
applicants under Section 105 or 105c (MCL 388.1705) pursuant to 
Section 22f(2)(c). 
97
  8. B.           11.13.12 
4) The district submits to the Michigan Department of Education a plan that 
shows progress  toward developing the technology infrastructure 
necessary for the implementation of pupil academic growth assessments 
by 2014-2015 pursuant to Section 22f(2)(d).  
5) The district supports opportunities for pupils to receive postsecondary 
credit while attending secondary school pursuant to Section 22f(2)(e). 
i. The district supports attendance of district pupils under the 
postsecondary enrollment options act, MCL 388.511 to 388.524, or 
under the career and technical preparation act, MCL 388.1901 to 
388.1913, consistent with the provisions under section 21b. 
ii. The district offers college-level equivalent courses, as defined in 
section 1471 of the revised school code, MCL 380.1471. 
iii. The district provides other opportunities to pupils that allow those 
pupils to gradua te with a high school diploma and also complete 
coursework that a postsecondary institution normally applies toward 
satisfaction of degree requirements. 
6) The district offers online instructional programs to all eligible pupils 
pursuant to Section 22f(2)(f). 
7) The District provides a link on the District’s home page to the URL for 
the MiSchool Data Portal which contain s the required dashboard 
indicators pursuant to Section 22f(2)(g).  If certain data elements for our 
District are unavailable from State data collections, we agree to provide 
those data in the form and manner determined by the MDE.   The 
following indicators are included: 
i. Graduation and dropout rates. 
ii. Average class size in grades kindergarten to 3. 
iii. College readiness as measured by Michigan merit  examination test 
scores. 
iv. Elementary and middle school MEAP scores. 
v. Teacher, principal and superintendent salary information including 
at least minimum, average and maximum pay levels. 
vi. General fund balance. 
vii. The total number of days of instruction provided. 
B. The Board of Education of Troy School District authorizes and directs its 
secretary to file this resolution with the State Aid and School Finance Office of 
the Michigan Department of Education. 
C. All resolutions and parts of resolutions insofar as they co nflict with the 
provisions of this resolution are hereby rescinded.

                          Board of Education Meeting to 5:45 p.m.

December 4, 2012, Board of Education meeting, it is  necessary for 
the Board of Education to change the start time of the meeting  to 
5:45 p.m. The Board will reconvene the meeting immediately 
following the closed session.

11. A.  CLOSED SESSION 
  CONSIDERATION:  Attorney – Client Privilege  
The Board must pass a resolution to go into closed session .  Following the 
passage of the resolution, the Board of Education will go into closed session to 
discuss an attorney – client privilege item .  No additional business will take 
place after this time, other than to officially adjourn the meeting. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
Be it therefore resolved that, as permitted in the Michigan Open Meetings Act, Public Act 
267, Section 8 (c), the Troy Board of Education shall go into closed session to discuss an 
attorney – client privilege item.
